9.4

The App Stores

By the end you can
  • Name what Google Play and the App Store require before an app can be published: developer accounts and their real costs, permanent app ids, and the technical rules builds must meet
  • Build a data inventory from an app's own code and use it to answer Apple's App Privacy details and Google Play's Data safety form
  • Explain both stores' rules for privacy policies and account deletion, and give an app an honest in-app path to both
  • Prepare a store listing within the stores' limits, and avoid the most common reasons apps are rejected in review
  • Configure eas submit for a testing track, and work through a release checklist from code to production
Key ideas
  • Two stores, two accounts: Apple's is yearly, Google Play's one-time, and new personal Play accounts run a closed test with testers before production (the lesson's table; check the official pages: they change). None is needed for this course.
  • Ids are permanent and on a domain you control (reversed); com.example is refused. Builds must meet each store's current technical rules: a current Expo SDK keeps you inside them.
  • Collected = leaves the phone and is kept. Answer Apple's App Privacy details and Google Play's Data safety from an inventory of the code AND every SDK, never from memory; redo it when the app changes.
  • Privacy policy and deletion: a policy link inside the app; apps that create accounts must let people start deleting them inside the app (Google also wants a web link). Shoreline Crew's API cannot delete, so its Your data screen says so and opens a request page.
  • Listing and review are your work, not eas submit's: texts within limits, real screenshots, a running server and a demo account for the reviewer. Most rejections are about completeness.
  • Testing tracks first: internal testing and TestFlight before production; eas submit sends a build to the track its submit profile names. The release checklist turns it into a routine.
Worked example: walk the deck before the manifest

The privacy forms ask what you COLLECT: data sent off the phone and kept. Walk the code first. Every place Shoreline Crew uses fetch, outside the tests:

grep -rnw "fetch" src --include="*.ts*" --exclude-dir=mocks --exclude-dir=test --exclude="*.test.*"
src/hooks/useRefreshOnFocus.ts:6: * Each time this screen comes back into view, fetch its stale answers again: the queries whose
src/api/client.ts:302:    response = await fetch(url, { ...init, headers });
src/api/client.ts:304:    // In the browser a failed fetch is a TypeError; on a phone, Expo's fetch throws its own error
src/components/UpdateStatus.tsx:8:// Which code this app runs, and (in preview and production builds) a way to fetch a new version
src/utils/photoFiles.web.ts:6:  const photo = await (await fetch(uri)).blob();
src/utils/photoFiles.web.ts:13:    return await (await fetch(uri)).blob();

Three comments and a local photo read: one real request, src/api/client.ts:302. So every row of the inventory asks two questions: does it go through that request, and does the server keep it? The email, sign-ups, logs and photos: yes and yes (collected, linked to the account). The location: never sent (the distance is worked out on the phone): not collected. Then the code you did not write: the map SDK on Android, and expo-updates' check once a project id is set (judgment rows, in the lesson).

Worked example: Milestone 1 of Copperline Couriers

Lesson 10.1's worked example is Copperline Couriers (brief 1), Milestone 1. Its rules live in the mock API, each with a test, before any screen exists:

npm test -- --watchAll=false src/mock-api/deliveries
 PASS  src/mock-api/deliveries.test.ts
  ✓ lists one rider's run sheet in the order of the drop-off windows

npx tsc --noEmit
src/utils/deliveries.ts:4:14 - error TS2741: Property 'returned' is missing in type '{ assigned: string; 'picked-up': string; delivered: string; failed: string; }' but required in type 'Record<DeliveryStatus, string>'.

                ~~~~~~~~~


Found 2 errors in the same file, starting at: src/utils/deliveries.ts:4

Not a mistake: a to-do list. Each error is a decision the new status needs (its words; which moves lead to it and from it). An array of statuses would have stayed silent, and the run sheet would have shown a stop with no label and no buttons.

  • ☐ Step 4: my names and ids (or com.example with a "to do"), slug, scheme, package name, app.config.test.ts
  • ☐ Step 5: my types next to Item, every union with its Record tables; sample data with one awkward record
  • ☐ Step 6: the mock API's rules, one file per resource, a test per rule, the must-never-break rule named in its test
  • ☐ Step 7: its own Git repository, pushed, a green CI run; the first time log line

10.2

Working on Real Mobile Projects

By the end you can
  • Estimate a mobile feature as a range, from a task breakdown that includes the work only phones add (two platforms, permissions, offline, builds, store review)
  • Choose between Expo Go, a development build, Continuous Native Generation and committed native folders, and explain when a local native module (Expo Modules API) is worth it
  • Work in a team with short branches, a phone-aware pull request template, CI and a release train that plans for store review
  • Keep an Expo app current: read npx expo install --check, npx expo-doctor and npm outdated, repair version drift with npx expo install --fix, and upgrade one SDK at a time
  • Write a learning plan for the months after the course, with your final project at its center
Key ideas
  • Estimates are ranges from a task breakdown. The work phones add: the happy path, the server, permissions, offline, two platforms, tests, privacy and the stores, shipping (update or build?). Three numbers per part; (optimistic + 4 × likely + pessimistic) ÷ 6; tell the client the range, the assumptions and what you depend on. Your time log is how your estimates improve.
  • Time that is not yours: build queues, store review (a rejection restarts the clock), a new Google Play account's closed test, other people. Plan it; check the stores' current pages before promising a date.
  • The platform ladder: Expo Go → development build → Continuous Native Generation (native folders generated from app.config.ts, never committed: where Shoreline Crew is) → committed native folders. A local native module (Expo Modules API, Swift and Kotlin) is a development-build feature.
  • Teams: short branches, a phone-aware pull request template (tested on which phones? native change or not? does it change what the app collects?), CI, kind and specific review, questions in four parts (goal, expected, actual word for word, tried). Release trains leave on time; JavaScript-only fixes can go out as updates between trains; native changes wait.
  • Keeping an app healthy: Expo releases several SDKs a year and Expo Go supports only the newest; the stores raise their rules every year. Health check: npx expo install --check, npx expo-doctor, npm outdated. "Latest" is not the right version for a native package; npx expo install --fix repairs drift. Upgrade at least yearly, one SDK at a time.
  • Dependency hygiene: fewer packages; native ones through npx expo install; read audits, never npm audit fix --force; commit the lockfile (CI uses npm ci); a calendar, not a mood.
Worked example: a range, and a green health check

The lesson's check-in photo, estimated in parts (example hours, for illustration): optimistic 5.5, likely 10, pessimistic 19. The three-point estimate:

node -e "console.log((5.5 + 4 * 10 + 19) / 6)"
10.75

What you tell the client is not "10.75 hours": "About two working days for the app, possibly up to three, once the server can accept the photo. The server part is not included, and the store privacy answers have to change before the release." A range, the assumptions and what you depend on. Then the health check on Shoreline Crew, green when the course was checked (the lesson's card; the check compares with Expo's current versions, so it can drift):

npx expo install --check
env: load .env.local
env: export EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL
Dependencies are up to date

  • Task 1: threePoint(), total(), forgotten(), sayRange() (the lesson's example numbers).
  • Task 2: classify() and report() on the lesson's npm outdated card. Task 3 (stretch): departure().

Found outdated dependencies
A) It waits for someone to answer the question
B) It fixes the package and passes
C) It passes with a warning
D) It prints the mismatch and exits with code 1: the pull request turns red and names the package
Q9
npm outdated in shoreline-crew lists TypeScript 7.0.2 as Latest. What is the right reading?
Package                                    Current  Wanted   Latest  Location                                                Depended by
typescript                                   6.0.3   6.0.3    7.0.2  node_modules/typescript                                 shoreline-crew
A) Update it now: Latest has the most fixes
B) npm outdated is broken for TypeScript
C) Leave it: SDK 58 pins TypeScript 6.0 (7.0 breaks npx expo lint, Lesson 1.3); "Latest" does not know about Expo, so wait until an SDK moves
D) Remove TypeScript and use JavaScript

A) Expo Go only, forever

C) Continuous Native Generation: android/ and ios/ are generated from app.config.ts and plugins (by EAS Build), never edited or committed
D) Ejected

A) Tonight, as an EAS Update

Glossary

TermMeaning
client briefA client's description of their people, must-haves, what they already have, device features and data, and what is out of scope
charterWorksheet section 0: who, what must work, what is out of scope and why, and what it does with no signal
walking skeletonThe thinnest whole app that runs end to end: every screen reachable, wired to real (mock) data, before polish (Milestone 2)
milestoneA checkpoint that ends with something working and a green CI run; seven in the final project
mock API (demo mode)The starter's src/mock-api: answers inside the app when EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL=demo, and in the tests through MSW
must-never-break ruleThe one rule the server keeps whatever a phone sends; it gets its own test
time logWorksheet section 16: estimate vs actual hours per milestone, and what surprised you
rubricTen rows, 0 (not yet) / 1 (meets) / 2 (strong); complete = no row at not yet
three-point (PERT) estimate(optimistic + 4 × likely + pessimistic) ÷ 6, said to a client as a range with assumptions
release trainA fixed release rhythm: what is merged and tested by the cut-off leaves; the rest waits for the next one
staged rollout / phased releaseReleasing to a share of users first (Google Play) / automatic updates spread over days (App Store)
Continuous Native Generation (CNG)android/ and ios/ generated from app.config.ts and plugins, never edited or committed
Expo Modules APIWriting your own native module in Swift and Kotlin with a TypeScript interface (a development-build feature)
version driftA package moved away from the version the Expo SDK expects; npx expo install --fix repairs it
decision recordA short document: context, options, decision, consequences, and when to revisit it

Self-check: the Part 3 Review

Twelve items on Modules 7 to 10, before the written final. Answers are in the answer key.

Q1
The logbook's pieces tally counted up to 42 with withSpring, sampled every few milliseconds. Why is that a problem, and what does the lesson use instead?
0 3 8 12 19 27 30 37 42 44 47 48 49 48 47 46 45 44 43 42 41 42
A) Nothing is wrong: springs feel alive
B) The phone is too slow for 60 frames
C) A spring overshoots its target: the tally showed totals that were never true (49, then 41). Numbers and progress get withTiming
D) Use setInterval for numbers
Q2
A tutorial's Gesture.Pan().onEnd(…) was copied into the course's Gesture Handler 3 hook config. What is the fix?
src/components/SwipeToRemove.tsx:42:5 - error TS2353: Object literal may only specify known properties, and 'onEnd' does not exist in type 'PanGestureConfig'.
A) The callbacks are properties of one config object with Gesture Handler 3's names: onActivate and onDeactivate; fix the first error and the second goes
B) Downgrade Gesture Handler
C) Add // @ts-ignore
D) Wrap the component in a Pressable
Q3
With only lottie-react-native installed, the phone is happy and tsc and lint are silent, but npx expo export --platform web stops with this. What is missing?
Error: Unable to resolve module @lottiefiles/dotlottie-react from ~/shoreline/shoreline-crew/node_modules/lottie-react-native/lib/module/LottieView/index.web.js: @lottiefiles/dotlottie-react could not be found within the project or in these directories:
A) A development build
B) The web player: install @lottiefiles/dotlottie-react with npx expo install
C) A Metro config change
D) Nothing: Lottie does not work on the web
Q4
A decorative icon is hidden from screen readers with aria-hidden="true". What does the type checker want?
src/components/Icon.tsx:35:6 - error TS2322: Type '{ name: { ios: "calendar"; android: "calendar_today"; web: "calendar_today"; } | { ios: "mappin.and.ellipse"; android: "location_on"; web: "location_on"; } | { ios: "person.2"; android: "group"; web: "group"; } | ... 7 more ... | { ...; }; size: number; tintColor: string | ... 1 more ... | NativeColorValue; "aria-hi...' is not assignable to type 'ViewProps'.
  Types of property '"aria-hidden"' are incompatible.
    Type 'string' is not assignable to type 'boolean | undefined'.
A) accessible="false"
B) A label instead
C) importantForAccessibility on iOS
D) A boolean: aria-hidden on its own (it means true) or aria-hidden={false}
Q5
Why does the course's Jest setup put Node's own fetch back before the tests run?
A) Node's fetch is faster
B) Jest has no fetch at all
C) Expo SDK 58 replaces fetch with a phone-only one, and MSW can only answer Node's
D) To reach the real shoreline-api
Q6
npx expo export --platform android in Lesson 8.4. What is this number for?
Android Bundled 19343ms node_modules/expo-router/entry.js (2084 modules)
...
› android bundles (1):
_expo/static/js/android/entry-a0fc6b8a5faced8847b7291067d2d3bd.hbc (5.5MB)
A) A measurement: write it down, change one thing (removing packages the app never uses), export again and compare
B) The store's size limit
C) How much memory the app uses
D) Nothing: bundle sizes do not matter on phones
Q7
npx expo-doctor before a build. What does it want, and why?
19/20 checks passed. 1 checks failed. Possible issues detected:
✖ Check that no duplicate dependencies are installed
Found duplicates for expo-server:
  ├─ expo-server@58.1.0 (at: node_modules/expo-server)
  └─ expo-server@57.0.3 (at: node_modules/expo-atlas/node_modules/expo-server)
A) Delete node_modules and reinstall
B) Ignore it: only one check failed
C) Pin expo-atlas to the newest version
D) One copy of each native module: an overrides entry in package.json tells npm which expo-server the other package gets
Q8
When does a project need a development build instead of Expo Go?
A) As soon as it has more than five screens
B) When it needs a native package Expo Go does not contain, its own name, icon and ids on the phone, or a config plugin's settings
C) Only for the App Store
D) Never: Expo Go runs everything
Q9
The update settings inside a build. What does file:fingerprint mean for an update after a native change?
EXUpdatesRuntimeVersion: 'file:fingerprint'
A) Updates ignore runtime versions
B) The fingerprint is a password for updates
C) The runtime version is a fingerprint of the native side: a native change gives a new runtime version, so it needs a new build; updates reach only builds with the same fingerprint
D) Every update makes a new fingerprint
Q10
The ids were changed to the client's own domain (org.marisolcrew) and the app config test fails. What is the test protecting, and what do you do?
 FAIL  ./app.config.test.ts
  ✕ builds Shoreline Dev from the development profile (186 ms)
  ✕ builds Shoreline Preview from the preview profile, with an API address (1 ms)
  ✕ refuses the production profile until it has an https API address (2 ms)
Q11
The evidence check on a final project ends like this. What does it mean?
4 passed, 9 to fix, 3 to check
NOT READY: fix the FIX lines above, then run this check again
A) The project failed the course
B) Evidence the rubric asks for is missing: fix every FIX line and run the check again; it lists evidence, it never scores
C) The tests failed
D) The project cannot be shown in the showcase
Q12
Your peer reviewer writes: "At the largest text size the late list cuts off the kayak names." How do you answer the point?
